# Beyond Data — formula register

Generated 19 Oct 2026 from `formula_register.json` (the diagram reads the JSON; edit that, then run `make_register_md.py`).

**Scale.** 100 arithmetic/physics · 90 regulation, tariff or standard read directly · 80 government dataset or statistic · 70 peer-reviewed coefficient transferred from another context · 60 derived with stated assumption · 50 operator or industry claim · 40 proxy · 20 judged · 0 gap

**Rule.** A quantity's confidence is the lowest of its inputs; a condition's is the lowest of its quantities; a match fails at its weakest link; burdens are recorded beside, never netted.

**Verification.** Independent re-verification 9–10 Oct 2026: 258 items re-opened; 195 confirmed, 37 confirmed with wording/page notes, 10 mismatches corrected, 6 not on page (citations fixed), 1 unreachable. See verification_report.md. Hunt 9 (public proxies) and hunt 10 (J.B. full-text uploads) folded in 10 Oct: see sources/09 and sources/10.

**Counts.** 167 quantities · 101 conditions under 10 headings · 15 opportunities quantified.

## Quantities

"own" is the confidence of the quantity's own coefficient and source; "upstream" is the lowest confidence of anything feeding it.

| `sewer_sink` | Sewer as heat source or sink | MW at the pipe | Q = flow × 4.18 × ΔT, within the temperature rules: sewage arriving at the plant ≥ 11 °C when used as a source (KWL design rule); waste entering a facility < 40 °C, non-domestic waste < 65 °C when used as a sink (Bylaw 299) | sewer_flow | Vancouver: ~45 MW city-wide, ~100 MW region-wide (KWL 2014, secondary); NEU uses 3.2 + 6.6 MW; access first-come first-served with a letter of support (UT-008); heat user pays access costs; per-pipe flow near a parcel is not published (only North Surrey 6 MW). Edmonton: no EPCOR sewer-heat programme or data found; non-contact cooling water to sanitary sewer needs EWSI written approval (Bylaw 18100 s.5.10); Edmonton: Blatchford's planned sewer heat exchange centre (two combined sewers under the future market area) was deferred after EPCOR reduced its sewer-flow forecasts (Blatchford Budget and Plans 2023–2026) — the first Edmonton evidence on sewer heat, and a caution | kwlSewer, gvsddUT008, gvsdd299, neuSewage, edm18100, blatchford2023, vanSewerCap2025, mvWDP2021, epcorDrainage2023 | 40 | 40 | rules in hand for Vancouver; flow at a parcel and all Edmonton figures are gaps | A sink the place offers |

| `heat_temp` | Heat temperature by cooling type | °C | air-cooled 25–35; liquid-cooled 50–60; two runs if unknown | cooling_type | 18 Oct 2026 (J.B. decisions, heat items 1–7; audit/heat_items_sources_2026-10-18.md): air-side return hot air 25–47 °C, chiller condenser 40–50 °C, chilled-water loop 10–20 °C (Yuan et al. 2025 Table 2; Oró et al. 2019); the tool keeps 25–35 °C as the typical air-cooled figure. Alissa p.332; Wahlroos p.1753; heat-rejection equipment per site from permits: rooftop condensers (1050 W Pender, 175 W Cordova), rooftop chiller (55 E 7th), P1 chiller room (555 W Hastings), L8 roof well (515 W Hastings); seen on site (J.B.): 55 E 7th lane deck with a Cummins generator enclosure and a packaged cooling unit piped into the building (rooftop chiller in BP-2019-00595 not photographed — keep both); 111 E 5th louvre bands and three rooftop vent stacks; Internet Centre two rooftop condensing units (corroborates the 2024 "two RTUs, AC only" permit); Qu EDM2 cooling units on a raised platform under a canopy, "Munters" casings (air-side or indirect evaporative suggested, not confirmed); Kyndryl Acheson cooling plant with pipe racks into the wall on the south side; Baxtel claim for Kyndryl: two 550-ton water-cooled chillers (≈3.9 MW thermal) — operator/directory claim ∣ 19 Oct 2026: liquid-cooled measured design 24 °C supply, 35–40 °C return (NREL ESIF); 50–60 °C remains unverified (secondary). | lorenz, vanPermits, fieldJB, yuan2025, oro2019, nrelESIF2019, upsiteASHRAE5 | 70 | 70 | in hand | Quality (temperature grade); Change in cooling; The heat is hot enough to make cooling |

Reuse is shown 'possible with a change' (heat pumps on the chilled-water loop or chiller condenser) and the figure 'not yet known'. Evidence: Oró et al. 2019 (return air up to 47 °C, condenser up to 50 °C; ERF on a primary-energy basis 25–45% / ~55%, not ISO ERF); Požgaj et al. 2025 (air-side 30% recovered by heat pumps in place of chillers, ERF 0.78–0.88, 108 kW hybrid); Ljungqvist et al. 2023 (theoretical upper limit with no heat pump, 0.50–0.66 at 13–18 °C); Yuan et al. 2025 review (Table 2 temperatures; its '50–70% recoverable' is cited to Singapore energy statistics — unsupported, not used). 13 Oct 2026: direct-to-chip 48–75 % (LBNL 2014; NREL), cold plate up to 94 % (Heydari 2024), hot-water cooled 80 % (Zimmermann 2012); air-cooled: no primary figure; the often-cited 97 % (Lu et al. 2011) describes electricity becoming heat, not capture — unverified at source. A water-cooled chiller plant rejects all heat to the condenser loop | lorenz, lbnlCapture, oro2019, pozgaj2025, ljungqvist2023, yuan2025 | 70 | 70 | in hand | Form; Change in cooling |
| `cop` | Heat-pump COP (Lorenz) | — | COP = η × T_lm,sink ÷ (T_lm,sink − T_lm,source), log-mean temperatures on both sides (sink supply/return; source in/out); η 0.40–0.50 for built systems (calibrated 13 Oct 2026), 0.50–0.60 upper bound | heat_temp, receiver_temp | checks: ~3 at 20→75 °C; 4.5–5.0 Odense; DEA Aug 2026 waste-heat heat pumps, annual average COP to 70 °C forward / 35 °C return: 3.1 at 1 MW (source 13→8 °C), 3.6 at 3 MW (15→8 °C), 4.7 at 10 MW (27→15 °C); air-source 3 MW 2.8; CALIBRATION 13 Oct 2026 (calibrate.py; sources/13): full Lorenz with η 0.50 gives 4.56 for Odense (27→15 / 40→75 °C) against realised 4.5 (2020) and 4.2 all-in (2021–22, η 0.49 / 0.46); Telia Helsinki snapshot 3.9 at 20/32 → ~80 °C implies η 0.39; Université Laval ~7 at ~15 → 40 °C implies η 0.53; ReUseHeat design 3.6 and Bahnhof simulation 3.7 imply 0.37 / 0.43. The simplified form used in the first attempt (sink supply ÷ (sink supply − source out)) under-predicts at large lifts (3.0–3.6 for Odense) and is retired; observed: Meta Odense heat pumps 27 → 70 °C, COP 4.5 in 2020 (14,020 MWh electricity, 230 TJ heat) and about 3.9 combined 2022–24 (Fjernvarme Fyn annual reports); tool range 3.0–4.5 | bytes2heat, dea, dfKatalog2022, fjfAars2020, lorenz, teliaHKI2024, ulavalCVD, blatchfordDG2025 | 70 | 70 | calibrated on six cases: η 0.39–0.53 built/operating | Quality (temperature grade) |

Measured (first field study, Phoenix, 36–169 MW): downwind air 0.7–0.9 °C warmer on average, up to 2.2 °C, detectable 100–500 m away; condenser discharge 8–14 °C above ambient. Placement: units on the roof warm street-level air less than units on walls (Berlin model: up to 0.6 K for wall units, smaller for rooftop; Jin et al. 2020). The effect is strongest at night: Phoenix AC waste heat added more than 1 °C at night at some locations, little by day (Salamanca et al. 2014). A land-surface preprint (+2 °C) is context only | sailor2026, jin2020, salamanca2014, dcLstPreprint | 60 | 50 | physics at the coil (100); at a neighbour derived (60); field range peer-reviewed but transferred from large desert sites (70) | — (input to others) |

Pools: water ≤ 37 °C by regulation, 25–28 °C recommended for competition pools (BC 2025). Ambient networks (Blatchford) take heat near ground temperature and lift it in each building. Hot water stored for taps must be at least 60 °C, and 49 °C anywhere in a recirculation loop (Vancouver Building By-law 2025 Book II, 2.6.1.12.(1), 2.6.1.1.(3)); so data-centre heat at 25–35 °C can preheat tap water but a heat pump or other source must finish it. Map records (15 Oct 2026, tool/receivers_*.json): Vancouver 353 — 27 recreation centres, 1 arena, 20 hospitals, 103 care homes, 202 schools (StatCan ODSRF v2 records no separate Vancouver pools; recreation-centre contents not recorded) — plus 641 non-market housing projects (City open data); Edmonton 475 — 161 recreation centres, 23 pools, 22 indoor arenas, 13 hospitals, 2 care homes, 254 schools. 72 health records have no location and are counted, not placed. s.3 ISO may aggregate data centres on the same or adjacent parcels under the same person or affiliate; s.5 'tethered' = large data centre whose access request is tied to a generating unit or storage facility of equal or greater capability; s.9 'bridged' = tethered data centre served before its generation is in service, for at most 3 years, with an ISO-set demand limit and an ISO-set limit on all bridged load (no MW figure in the regulation — the 1,600 MW pool is an AESO process figure, not statute); Alberta (Guide to Groundwater Authorization 2023 §6.2): open loop with no water loss and return to the same water body = Water Act "activity" → approval; different water body or any loss → licence; closed loop → none. Flow tier for the aquifer test: Q(m³/day) = heat_kW ÷ (1.16 × ΔT) × 24; a 1 MW loop at ΔT 5 K ≈ 4,100 m³/day → top tier | ground | Alberta EPA fact sheet, Ground Source Heat Exchange Systems (8 Oct 2025; 2024 edition identical on these points): open-loop systems "are regulated through a Water Act authorization" obtained before construction; application must assess the aquifer's capability to circulate the required quantity continuously, effects on groundwater quantity, temperature and quality, and on household, agricultural and existing licence holders; complex systems need an APEGA groundwater specialist report per the Guide to Groundwater Authorization; water in an open loop cannot be used for another purpose or temporarily discharged; open-loop wells drilled by a Class A driller, return through a separate return well; horizontal closed loop above the water table and vertical closed loop above the base of groundwater protection need no Water Act authorization (Class F installer); below the base of groundwater protection the AER regulates under the Geothermal Resource Development Act; applications via DRAS. Directive (11 Dec 2018) sets the construction standards; Guide App. 2 aquifer-test tiers by daily pumping rate: ≤10 m³/day 2+2 h, no observation well, no APEGA report (§2.2); 10–35 m³/day 24+24 h, 0–1 obs well; 35–65 m³/day 24+24 h, 1; 65–265 m³/day 48+48 h, 1–2; >265 m³/day 72+72 h, 1–2 obs wells, full §2 report by an APEGA groundwater practitioner; maximum daily rate = lesser of test rate and Q20 (20-year sustainable yield); CSA C448 referenced for installation; temporary discharge needs the director's authorisation | wsa, abDirective, abGSHE, abGuide, bcUCAs1, aucCoaldale2025 | 90 | 80 | BC and Alberta both confirmed from the regulator (data/); Alberta open loop = Water Act authorization with a temperature-impact assessment — a pass/partial condition, not a veto | Utility regulation |

## How the tool reads a match (decided 13 Oct 2026; see tool_logic_decisions.md)

No verdict and no score. Each option gets a trade-off table: every party, every row, in its own unit, never summed or ranked. Facts are shared; the weighting belongs to whoever is deciding.

### Parties

- **Operator** — owns or runs the data centre
- **Host / landlord** — owns the building or land it sits in
- **Receiver** — takes heat, power, water or space: a network, a building, a greenhouse, a shelter
- **Utility** — electricity, water, sewer and district-energy operators
- **City** — zoning, permits, public land, enforcement of conditions
- **Neighbours** — people who live and work within reach today
- **The public passing by** — the street edge, security zones, surveillance as experienced
- **Ratepayers and taxpayers** — who pays costs passed through tariffs or budgets
- **Workers** — people who build and run it
- **Future residents** — people who live with a 40-year building and network after today's neighbours
- **The climate** — carbon, counted on average and marginal factors
- **First Nations (rights holders)** — Musqueam, Squamish and Tsleil-Waututh (Vancouver); Treaty 6 nations (Edmonton, Amiskwacîwâskahikan). The tool describes which processes and rights apply to an option, with sources, and never speaks for them; their gains and losses are left for the Nations to state. Decided for now; J.B. may revisit.

### What it would take to move a condition

Proposed by Claude (13 Oct 2026), for J.B. to check: each condition lists what it would take to move it, cheapest first. Only 'not movable' is a hard constraint; everything else is a trade-off at a price. A limit set by a user is respected, and what relaxing it would open is shown separately.

- `choice` **a different option** — changes with the design or the site, not with a price
- `money` **money** — equipment, pipe, enclosure, a higher price paid
- `time` **time and process** — permits, rezoning, licences, contracts, consultation
- `policy` **a change in policy or law** — tariffs, by-laws, provincial or federal rules
- `infra` **city-scale infrastructure** — substations, networks, transmission
- `physics` **not movable** — physics and climate; the ceiling everything else works under

### Exchanges, both directions

| exchange | data centre → place | place → data centre |
|---|---|---|
| Heat and cold | `heat_captured`, `heat_delivered`, `hourly_overlap`, `stored_heat` | `free_cooling_hours`, `ground`, `sewer_sink`, `surface_water_sink` |
| Power | `spare_capacity`, `flexible_load`, `backup_hours` | `electricity_price`, `grid_headroom`, `rooftop_solar`, `self_gen_rules` |
| Water | `rainwater`, `blowdown` | `water_supply` |
| Space | `offerable_area` | `existing_building`, `zoning_status`, `row_cost` |
| Materials | `copper_tonnes`, `ewaste`, `rare_earth` | `existing_building` |
| Sound | `noise_level` | `ambient_cover` |

### Lifecycle

| phase | duration | basis | conf |
|---|---|---|---|
| Planning, permits, grid | 1–3 (Vancouver rezoning ~15 months to decision; grid can add 12–36 months) | vanRezoneTime, jlarc2024, bchTransmission, aesoPerf | 70 |
| Construction | 1–1.5 per building; 2–3 to operation | jlarc2024, ieaEnergyAI | 60 |
| Ramp-up | first 1–4 years at 1,300–4,400 full-load hours of the heat pump | tallaghtD22, fjfAars2020, fjfAars2124 | 70 |
| Mature year (the table) | — | — | 70 |
| Server refresh | every 5–6 years (LBNL average 5.7 in 2023) | lbnlLife, tenK_life | 80 |
| Contracts and leases | colocation 2–5 years; scale 5–10+; heat contracts 10 + 5 (Stockholm) | dlrContracts, exergiAR2023 | 50 |
| Plant replacement | chillers and heat pumps ~20–25 years (ASHRAE medians) | ashraeLife | 80 |
| Network life | 30 years at 120 °C; 50 only below 115 °C (EN 253) | en253Life | 80 |
| Closure or reuse | case evidence only: heat-pump recoveries 14 of 19 ended (Sweden); Ericsson Vaudreuil closed within a year; a Sobeys data centre demolished (Edmonton 2021) | lygnerud, velkova2019, edmPermits | 50 |

### How options are made

1. build every combination the tool knows (size, position on the parcel or another parcel, adjacency, technology, both cities, in or out of town)
2. report the gates first — grid connection (r_grid) and zoning use (z_use): a blocked gate is named before any exchange is weighed, and the exchanges still show as "if the gate moved"
3. set aside options that break a user limit — and keep them for the "if you moved this limit" panel
4. drop options worse than another on every row for every party (not a trade-off)
5. compare positions in the building as options where the user has not fixed one (basement · ground · upper floor · roof), each with its floor load, fuel, flood, vibration and street-front rows
6. pick as many as the user asked for, as different as possible on the rows the user emphasised
7. let the user try things on any option — "what if we added…" — whether an opportunity for the place (a seasonal heat store, cooling for neighbours, batteries for the grid), something the place offers (reclaimed water, neighbours' greywater, the ground), or a technology choice (solar, water recovery, cleaner backup power) — and show what each changes and the rules in each city
8. show them side by side, in no order, each with its full trade-off table
9. run each option through the four futures (as planned · tenant changes cooling · tenant leaves · receiver leaves) and show what changes

Preferences: **Number of options** — how many suggestions the user wants; **Limits** — non-negotiables, e.g. no rezoning; ≤ 45 dB at the nearest bedroom at night; **Emphasis** — which rows the options should differ on; never a weight; **Lens** — which party's view is read first; every row stays visible

## Condition states

Condition states (met / with a change / not met / not applicable or declined / not yet known / not judged) describe single conditions only. There is no match verdict: decided 13 Oct 2026 — the tool lays out trade-offs by party and does not rank or score options. Wording is suggested for J.B. to accept.

- `pass` **works** — every condition for the match passes
- `partial` **works with a change** — at least one condition passes only if a named change is made; the blocker is named
- `fail` **doesn't work** — a condition fails and no change within the match removes it; the blocker is named
- `na` **not applicable or declined** — the condition does not bear on this match, or the receiver has said no — a valid answer, not a failure
- `cannot` **not yet known** — a figure or clause the condition needs is missing; the tool names what is needed (operator floor price, parcel MW headroom, BC marginal factor, per-activity intensity)
- `unassessed` **not judged** — the run has not reached this condition yet

## Opportunities and the quantities that size them

- **District heat to a network**: `heat_delivered`, `homes_heated`, `window`, `distribution_cost`, `tes_volume` (weakest 50)
- **Hospital or campus anchor**: `heat_delivered`, `receiver_demand`, `horizons` (weakest 50)
- **Seasonal storage in the ground**: `stored_heat`, `storage_footprint`, `licence_flag`, `btes` (weakest 50)
- **Aquaculture / insects / algae**: `aquaculture_tonnes`, `insect_check`, `algae_kg`, `heat_temp` (weakest 50)
- **Warming centre, refuge, clean-air room**: `refuge_people`, `clean_air_volume`, `cooling_centre_people`, `backup_hours`, `crossing_type` (weakest 60)
- **Reserved capacity and batteries to the grid**: `spare_capacity`, `flexible_load`, `backup_hours`, `lrmc_bc`, `battery`, `ups_flex` (weakest 40)
- **EV charging hub**: `ev_stalls`, `ev_area`, `ev_exemption`, `grid_headroom` (weakest 50)
- **Meanwhile uses on land held for growth**: `offerable_area`, `horizons` (weakest 60)
- **Rainwater, condensate and water reuse**: `rainwater`, `blowdown` (weakest 50)
- **Material bank: copper**: `copper_tonnes`, `ewaste`, `rare_earth` (weakest 50)
- **Distributed servers as heaters**: `distributed_servers_homes`, `servers` (weakest 40)
- **New district with the data centre as heat plant**: `heat_delivered`, `homes_heated`, `heat_density`, `capex_split` (weakest 50)
- **Greenhouse**: `greenhouse_area`, `heat_temp` (weakest 50)
- **Sewage heat as comparator source**: `sewage_heat` (weakest 80)
- **Cooling for neighbours**: `absorption`, `heat_temp` (weakest 50)

## Sources by term

source_roles (added 12 Oct 2026): for each source, the term of the formula it supplies, its role (coefficient · bound · proxy · check · site fact · rule · data · method · gap) and its own confidence on the register scale; verified=false marks a placement that is Claude's reading of the coefficient text rather than an explicit tie, for J.B. to confirm. A quantity's own confidence is not recomputed from these: it was set in verification and can sit below its sources where the transfer or an assumption is the weak link (status says why), or above a source used only as a check or bound. The md lists every quantity where own confidence differs from the lowest coefficient-role source, for review.

### Own confidence vs sources (for review)

- `floor_area` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — Edmonton open; Vancouver via licensed BCA extract — field list confirmed from BCA user guide (data/BCA_…User_Guide.pdf); extract itself pending Abacus access; City property-tax report has no area field (schema checked) — fallback is footprint × storeys
- `it_load_outside` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — operator claims for the figure used (conf 60); government figures kept as context
- `servers` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — in hand
- `heat_captured` own 90, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — in hand (measurement boundary defined 13 Oct 2026)
- `pue` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 100 — range in hand: an estimate (LBNL 2023) and an industry survey (Uptime 2025), not measured here; confidence 80, not 100 (M104, 19 Oct 2026)
- `backup_hours` own 90, lowest coefficient-role source 50 — in hand
- `grid_co2` own 90, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — average intensities confirmed from the national inventory (both provinces, same basis, 1990–2024); Alberta displacement schedule from the regulator; BC marginal factor remains unpublished
- `refrigerant_co2e` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — default ranges from the IPCC inventory guidelines (read 19 Oct 2026); no Canadian or manufacturer figure for these chillers (M98)
- `noise_level` own 50, lowest coefficient-role source 40 — method in hand; manufacturer-rated levels (AHRI 370 / ISO 9614 / ISO 8528-10) for a starter set; site schedule still needed
- `light_at_window` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — standard transferred
- `cooling_centre_people` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — derived
- `offerable_area` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 20 — ground floor by rule (90) in three Vancouver zones; support block from plans (60); growth space judged (20)
- `receiver_demand` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 75 — proxy
- `greenhouse_area` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — transferred
- `aquaculture_tonnes` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 50 — confirmed (peer-reviewed full texts; Norwegian/Romanian climates transferred)
- `sewage_heat` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — flow gap
- `price_floor` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — derived
- `price_ceiling` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — derived; Edmonton: pricing principle confirmed from the City report (90), tariff pending
- `capex_split` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 50 — confirmed (full texts)
- `build_cost` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 50 — confirmed (textbook, 2010–2017 USD data; local CAD/ft² industry)
- `horizons` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 50 — primary sources found (13 Oct 2026)
- `lead_time` own 90, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — confirmed
- `grid_headroom` own 90, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — confirmed (regulator and utility documents read in full); MW headroom at a downtown Vancouver parcel is explicitly nil for new feeder positions from fiscal 2027 until the WES is in service (July 2032) — the tool reports this as a hard timing constraint, not a judgement
- `sewer_sink` own 40, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — rules in hand for Vancouver; flow at a parcel and all Edmonton figures are gaps
- `surface_water_sink` own 40, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — rules in hand; capacity is site-specific and unmodelled
- `self_gen_rules` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 50 — in hand
- `electricity_price` own 90, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — in hand (2026 tariffs)
- `water_supply` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — Vancouver in hand; Edmonton partial
- `existing_building` own 40, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — precedents in hand; stock at a parcel needs the parcel tables (N1–N7)
- `ambient_cover` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — Vancouver in hand; Edmonton to recheck
- `rezoning_price` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — Vancouver and Edmonton fees and durations in hand; Edmonton arterial-road assessment rates not retrieved
- `consultation` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — triggers read directly (90); whether an option meets one depends on land tenure, water source and on-site generation (60); Alberta 2013 policy read
- `people_affected` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — in hand (tool v18, 16 Oct 2026)
- `jobs` own 50, lowest coefficient-role source 60 — range from two planning records (50); applicant figures are claims
- `construction_time` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — in hand (transferred); local derivation possible for Edmonton
- `pv_facade` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — in hand (NRCan vertical values, 15 Oct 2026)
- `computing_served` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 50 — read
- `heat_plume_dT` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — physics at the coil (100); at a neighbour derived (60); field range peer-reviewed but transferred from large desert sites (70)
- `recirculation_flag` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — geometric flag from the ASHRAE handbook (approximate); no recirculation percentage formula
- `uhi_context` own 80, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — read
- `air_screen_1h` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — derived with stated assumptions
- `air_vs_objective` own 70, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — objectives read (90); background partial
- `air_permit_trigger` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — rule read; applicability unsettled
- `homes_heated_2050s` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 80 — derived with a stated assumption
- `refuge_after_quake` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 90 — derived from rules read
- `power_bill_effect` own 60, lowest coefficient-role source 70 — utility modelling of policy options (not decided); no source at in-city scale

## Sources

Source ids added 9–11 Oct are listed first with their citations; ids from the first register (hunts 1–8) follow, with their full reports in sources/01–08.

- `vbbl2025_2713scope` — VBBL 2025 Book II Art. 2.7.1.3: (1) treated non-potable water may serve Column A or B uses; (2) "An alternate water source system shall use treated non-potable water in lieu of potable water for all of the uses set out in Column A" (water closets, urinals, trap primers); cooling-tower make-up is a Column B (optional) use for rainwater and clear-water waste. ISO text and Maekawa 1968 not read (paywalled) — https://www.mdpi.com/2079-3197/9/12/129/html

PDF print 16 Oct 2026, read by OCR): Phase 1 rolled out 4 Jun 2025 with a 1,200 MW interim large-load connection limit and an allocation process; Phase 2A develops a connection process for large loads that self-supply on site, enable net new transmission-connected generation, or need MW allocations ("bring your own generation"); Proposed BYOG Process posted 26 Jun 2026; at 8 Sep 2026 the process was still being finalized, with application intake expected in fall 2026; a final proposal waits on a data-centre regulation enabled by Bill 8 (fall 2025). Working group included eStruxture, IREN, Beacon AI Centers and generators — https://aesoengage.aeso.ca/pre-engagement-phase-ii-large-load-integration
- `aesoBYOG2026` — AESO, Large Load Integration Phase 2A — Proposed BYOG Process (26 Jun 2026, proposal, not final). Applies to transmission-connected loads asking for 75 MW or more of demand transmission service (DTS), site-aggregated; excludes loads under 75 MW, distribution-connected load and storage. First intake gas-fired generation only, "net new"; tethering ratio: generator output at 25 °C, 40% humidity equals DTS contract capacity. Data centres must show municipal zoning or area-structure-plan approval and accept the data-centre connection requirements. DTS commitment fee $15k/MW (refundable). Bridging before the generator runs: at most 20/30/40% of the DTS request by generator milestone, 1,600 MW across all intakes, up to 3 years; bridging load is fully interruptible, reduced before all other non-firm load to avoid a shortfall, curtailment scheme TBD (pro rata, rotation or combination); suspended for failing to curtail — https://aesoengage.aeso.ca/pre-engagement-phase-ii-large-load-integration
- `aesoP2Aupd2026` — AESO, Large Load Integration Phase 2A BYOG Process Update, stakeholder session deck (18 Jun 2026, updated 24 Jun 2026). About 20 GW of data-centre load requested each quarter since Q2 2025 (19.2 GW Q2 2026). Phase 2 is modular: 2A BYOG, 2B ISO tariff, 2C "Grid Partners" (flexible load structures, operating procedures, market products), not yet set. Load management: data centres prioritized above other loads for reduction under supply-related conditions; in standard operation, if firm load shed is needed in an emergency, data centres reduce before all other firm loads; procedures (pro rata, blocks, rotations) to be developed. 74.9 MW applications rose from 9 (Jun 2025) to 18 (Jun 2026) in the 25–75 MW band; AESO may lower the 75 MW threshold. Distribution-connected large loads bypass BYOG; AESO has limited tools for them. Final process was planned for late July–early August 2026 — https://aesoengage.aeso.ca/pre-engagement-phase-ii-large-load-integration
- `aesoDCupd2025` — AESO Update on Data Centres (Sep 2025): over 20,000 MW of data-centre load on the AESO project list; Phase 1 qualified 15 projects / 5 developers / 4,800 MW and assigned 1,200 MW to 3 projects / 2 developers, contracts pending execution (deadline extended to Oct 2025 for the provincial levy engagement); draft technical connection requirements for transmission-connected data centres posted 22 Aug 2025, final requirements planned Nov 2025 — https://aesoengage.aeso.ca/pre-engagement-phase-ii-large-load-integration
- `alta117_2026` — Data Centre Regulation, Alta Reg 117/2026 under the Electric Utilities Act (King's Printer extract, current as of 9 Jun 2026; J.B. PDF 16 Oct 2026). s. 1: data centre includes hosting/cloud, digital asset mining, AI/ML, content delivery. s. 2: "large data centre" = maximum demand directly from the transmission system of 75 MW or more, or a lower threshold the AESO sets for reliability. s. 3: AESO may aggregate integrated data centres on the same or adjacent parcels with the same owner or affiliate. s. 5: a large data centre may be "tethered" to new or added generating units or energy storage able to supply at least its maximum demand, reliably and predictably (the Regulation does not limit the fuel; AESO's first intake does). s. 6: tethered requests processed first. s. 7: AESO may require site control, municipal zoning approvals, financing, permits or water licences. s. 8: no service above the tethered supply. s. 9: bridged service before the supply runs only once the plant is approved by the AUC, max 3 years, AESO may cap each and all bridged loads and suspend for unmet criteria. s. 10: inadequate supply — bridged data centres reduced before all other market participants; mandatory load shed — large data centres reduced before all others. s. 12: review by 31 May 2030, expires 31 May 2035 — https://open.alberta.ca/publications/2026_117
- `edm7140sun` — Edmonton Zoning Bylaw 20001 (Charter Bylaw 20001 as adopted, effective 1 Jan 2024; City PDF, OCR text, J.B. download 14 Oct 2026; later amendments not checked), s. 7.140 Special Information Requirements, subs. 9 Sun Shadow Impact Study: the Development Planner MAY require one only where the development is a Discretionary Development (9.1); prepared by an Alberta P.Eng. or architect (9.2.1); shadows every 3 hours between sunrise and sunset, Mountain Standard Time, on 21 Mar, 21 Jun, 21 Sep and 21 Dec (9.2.3); impact judged as the difference between the maximum massing the zone allows and the proposed massing (9.3.1); may refuse or require redesign (9.3.2–9.3.3). No shadow clause found in the standard industrial zones (BE, IM, IH); shadow clauses appear for towers in RL and MU (in compliance with 7.140), UI height variances, and special areas (HOR, CMU, AED, RTCC, ALA); 3.50 Energy and Technology Park mentions shading only as tree planting. s. 2.140 IH lists permitted uses only (Major Industrial; Minor Industrial limited to those existing at 1 Jan 2024) and no discretionary uses, so the study cannot be required there; the bylaw names no data-centre use (Minor/Major Industrial definitions, Part 8), so the use class is for the Development Planner to determine. Search of the full text for shad/shadow/sun — https://zoningbylaw.edmonton.ca/

Applies in Alberta and BC. ECCC announced targeted amendments 9 Nov 2025 (not yet in the consolidation) — https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/SOR-2022-140/FullText.html

Reg. 295/2023, Schedule 3 Table 1: base carbon intensity of diesel fuel 94.38 gCO2e/MJ (gasoline 93.67); General Regulation 282/2023: 30% reduction by 2030; BC requirements page (updated 6 Mar 2026): diesel target CI 79.28 (2024), 77.11 (2025), 74.94 (2026) to 66.07 (2030); renewable content in diesel 8% from 2025; from 1 Apr 2025 eligible renewable diesel must be produced in Canada (yearly targets from the government page, regulation copies read were older) — https://www2.gov.bc.ca/gov/content/industry/electricity-alternative-energy/transportation-energies/renewable-low-carbon-fuels/requirements

C 1.2.1.5(2)) — https://bylaws.vancouver.ca/consolidated/14419.PDF
- `mvBylaw1329` — Metro Vancouver Non-Road Diesel Engine Emission Regulation Bylaw No. 1329, 2021: applies to non-road diesel engines of 25 hp or more, including stationary engines; emergency engines (incl. emergency generators) are exempt from Parts 3–6 and 9 (registration, fees, tier rules) but must be maintained to manufacturer procedures, keep exhaust away from building openings and air intakes, and keep records; no limit on testing hours. - `vanDeepGeo` — No published temperature-at-depth assessment for Vancouver / the Lower Mainland was found. Heat flow west of the Coast Plutonic Complex is low, about 44 mW/m² (Lewis, Judge & Souther 1978, abstract); GSC OF 6914 (Grasby et al. 2012) names southern BC volcanic belts (Garibaldi) as EGS targets; Geoscience BC Report 2015-11 covers volcanic-belt sites 100+ km away (Meager Creek 180–280 °C). Edmonton 90 °C at 2.5 km confirmed (Majorowicz & Grasby 2021, Table A1) — https://download-telecharger.services.geo.ca/pub/nrcan_rncan/publications/STPublications_PublicationsST/291/291488/gid_291488.pdf
- `smoke2026` — Wildfire smoke: Alberta counts a smoke day as daily PM2.5 above 15 µg/m³; northern Alberta stations had more than 100 smoke days in 2023 (Edmonton values only in a chart); Metro Vancouver had a 14-day wildfire smoke advisory in 2018 and two multi-day smoke advisories in 2023; smoke affected Metro Vancouver air in seven of the last ten years (2024 summary). No official annual smoke-hour counts for either city — https://www.alberta.ca/air-indicators-wildfire-smoke
- `nfcAB2023_4313` — National Fire Code – 2023 Alberta Edition (J.B. download 16 Oct 2026), Tables 4.3.13.4.-A and -B read by Claude: identical to NFC 2020 — Classes II and IIIA tanks for emergency generators, first storey 200 000 L per protected storage room (tanks > 2 500 L); basement and storeys above the first, 20 000 L (tanks > 2 500 L) or 45 000 L (tanks > 20 000 L). Bylaw 17943 (2022 consolidation, read by Claude): variable rate $0.0269/kWh — https://blatchfordutility.ca/2026-rates-blatchford-renewable-energy/

Operator page (Jan 2025): over 100 homes connected. Earlier plan (2020–2023) had forecast 1,409 customers by 2023. - `buffa2019` — Buffa et al. 2019, 5th generation district heating and cooling systems: a review of existing cases in Europe, Renewable and Sustainable Energy Reviews 104:504–522: FGZ Friesenberg, Zürich (2014), "an HE is installed for direct heat recovery from a data center" (p. 515), network supply 8–28 °C; in summer the warm pipe reaches about 30 °C after the data centre, limiting free cooling downstream; borehole count inconsistent in the paper (332 vs 153). FGZ factsheet (Swiss Federal Office of Energy support): data-centre waste heat 9 MW, 27 GWh/a, 450 boreholes as seasonal storage (18 GWh); not stated as measured — https://doi.org/10.1016/j.rser.2018.12.059
- `bfe2023dc` — BFE/EnergieSchweiz 2023, Abwärmenutzung von Rechenzentren (Kulemann et al.): data-centre heat can enter a low-temperature network without a boost if taken out above 32 °C (p. 20); fictional 20 MW IT case: 48% of waste heat used with a 25 MW network, 12% with a 5 MW network (p. 24); data-centre waste heat has hardly had a price (p. 26); operators plan over 10–15 years, heat networks over 30–40 (p. 5). Canton of Zürich RRB 1340/2025: waste heat above 2 GWh must be offered to third parties at production cost — https://www.bfe.admin.ch/

- `ljungqvist2023` — Ljungqvist, Risberg, Toffolo, Vesterlund 2023, A realistic view on heat reuse from direct free air-cooled data centres, Energy Conversion and Management: X 20:100473 (peer-reviewed, open access; J.B. upload, read 18 Oct 2026): theoretical upper limit, no heat pump; ERF 0.50–0.66 at 30–50 °C exhaust, reuse at 13–18 °C, Luleå — https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ecmx.2023.100473

- `cec2024ESS` — CSA C22.1:24 Canadian Electrical Code, Section 64 (read 19 Oct 2026, J.B. library copy): in force BC 4 Mar 2025, Alberta 1 Apr 2025 (Alberta Municipal Affairs notice, Apr 2025). Rule 64-1002: battery energy storage over 1 kWh must be certified (Rule 2-024), installed to the manufacturer's instructions, and — except in an electrical equipment vault — not where the floor is more than 23 m above grade or below the lowest level of egress. Rule 64-926: outdoor batteries at least 3 m from a means of egress and 1.5 m from windows, vents and doors, unless evaluated to ANSI/CAN/UL 9540A and installed to the manufacturer's instructions. Rule 64-902: plaque showing the system and its disconnects. Appendix B: the ESS definition does not include a UPS (covered by CSA C22.2 No. 107.3); batteries in general follow Rules 64-800–64-820 (ventilation if they vent gas, access by authorized persons only). 20 kWh / 80 kWh caps apply to residential occupancies only (Rule 64-1100). Neither Vancouver Fire By-law 14419 nor Edmonton Fire Rescue Services Bylaw 15309 (consolidated 1 Jan 2025, read) has battery or fuel provisions — data/raw/codes/CEC_2024_note.txt; data/raw/ab/ma_notice_CEC2024_2025-04.pdf; data/raw/edm/bylaw/C15309_fire_2025-01-01.pdf

- `lbnlLargeLoad2025` — LBNL (Jan 2025), Electricity rate designs for large loads: mechanisms (stranded assets, forecast error, avoided charges) and protections (minimum bills, exit fees, collateral, contributions); no quantified rate effect — https://eta-publications.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2025-01/electricity_rate_designs_for_large_loads_evolving_practices_and_opportunities_final.pdf

- `sailor2026` — Sailor, Samareh Abolhassani & Martin 2026, Data center waste heat as an emerging urban thermal hazard: first field measurements (ASME J. Engineering for Sustainable Buildings and Cities 7(2) 024501, peer-reviewed short communication; read 19 Oct 2026): vehicle traverses at four Phoenix facilities of 36–169 MW: downwind air up to 2.2 °C warmer, average 0.7–0.9 °C warmer than upwind; detectable 100–500 m from the perimeter depending on size, wind and layout; air-cooled condenser discharge 8–14 °C above ambient (cited); neighbourhood sensitivity about 1 °C per 100 W/m² (cited). Hot-desert summer, large facilities: transfer to a 2.5 MW in-city site with caution — doi 10.1115/1.4071922; data/raw/papers/sailor2026_dc_waste_heat.pdf
- `abConsultPolicy2013` — Government of Alberta, Policy on Consultation with First Nations on Land and Natural Resource Management, 2013 (amended 1 Apr 2020; read 19 Oct 2026): applies to Crown decisions on land and natural resource management that may adversely impact Treaty rights or traditional uses on provincial Crown lands; 'Matters Not Subject to this Policy' include 'Accessing private lands to which First Nations do not have a right of access for exercising their Treaty rights and traditional uses'; proponents (industry, municipal governments) may be delegated procedural aspects; the consultation office assesses adequacy — data/raw/ab/ir-policy-consultation-first-nations-land-resources-2013-amended-2020.pdf
- `duvenhageKroger1996` — Duvenhage & Kröger 1996, The influence of wind on the performance of forced draught air-cooled heat exchangers (J. Wind Eng. Ind. Aerodyn. 62, 259–277; peer-reviewed, numerical; read 19 Oct 2026): cross winds cut the air delivered by up-wind fans; winds along the bank's long axis raise hot-plume recirculation along its sides; a raised fan platform sees more wind and more up-wind recirculation; modelled system effectiveness 83.5% (cross wind, mostly lost fan flow; 3.1% from recirculation) and 89.5% (long-axis wind, mostly recirculation) — an industrial bank with a 10.7 m fan platform — data/raw/papers/duvenhage_kroger_1996.pdf
- `molla2026` — Molla 2026, In my backyard? Digital heat islands and the environmental justice of AI infrastructure in Phoenix (Climate Physics and Atmospheric Science 3–4, 100016; peer-reviewed short communication; read 19 Oct 2026): 46 data-centre buildings; land-surface temperature 39.74 °C within 1 km vs 39.26 °C citywide (≈ +0.5 °C), NDVI 0.15 vs 0.17, disproportionate proximity to low-income and minority populations; exploratory, 'does not establish causality' — data/raw/papers/molla2026_digital_heat_islands.pdf
- `jin2020` — Jin, Schubert, Salim & Schneider 2020, Impact of air conditioning systems on the outdoor thermal environment during summer in Berlin (Int. J. Environ. Res. Public Health 17, 4645; peer-reviewed, modelled): wall-mounted AC outdoor units raise near-surface air temperature up to 0.6 K, more at night and in urban areas; rooftop units have a smaller effect — data/raw/papers/jin2020_berlin_ac.pdf
- `salamanca2014` — Salamanca et al. 2014, Anthropogenic heating of the urban environment due to air conditioning (J. Geophys. Res. Atmospheres; peer-reviewed, WRF + building energy model, 10-day Phoenix heat event; read 19 Oct 2026): AC waste heat's mean daytime effect near the surface was negligible, but at night it raised 2 m air temperature by more than 1 °C at some urban locations and increased cooling demand — data/raw/papers/salamanca2014_phoenix_ac.pdf
- `ashraeCh45` — ASHRAE Handbook — HVAC Applications (SI, 2015), Ch. 45 Building Air Intake and Exhaust Design (read 19 Oct 2026, J.B. library copy): roof-mounted equipment draws air near the roof that can be 'considerably hotter', reducing the capacity of cooling towers and air-cooled condensers; obstructions next to horizontal-discharge units 'can drastically reduce equipment performance'; exhaust-to-intake recirculation 'can be a serious problem' for equipment with intake and exhaust on the same housing, caused by adverse wind, an upwind obstruction or a close downwind obstruction; flat-roof recirculation zones (Wilson 1979, approximate): R = Bs^0.67 × BL^0.33, upwind roof-edge zone height Hc = 0.22R at Xc = 0.5R, length Lc = 0.9R, downwind cavity Lr = R; cooling towers kept as far as possible from intakes, operable windows and outdoor public areas (Legionella) — data/raw/papers/ashrae_hvacapps_ch45_intake_exhaust.pdf; ASHRAE Handbook — Fundamentals (2025) Ch. 24 Airflow Around Buildings: orient cooling towers to prevailing winds; leeward recirculation and reentrainment — data/raw/papers/ashrae_fund_ch24_airflow.pdf
- `squamishNEU2010` — Compass Resource Management with FVB Energy and Hemmera, District of Squamish Neighbourhood Energy Utility feasibility study, final report, 15 Jun 2010: levelised cost over 30 years (pp. 27, 42); WACC 7.6% (5.6% real), ROE 10% (8% real) regulated by BCUC, debt 6%, 60/40; depreciation DPS 1.5%/yr, ETS 3%/yr (p. 44) — https://squamish.ca/assets/Land-Use-Policies/61bbc09bea/Squamish-NEU-Final-Feasibility-Report.pdf
- `energiforsk2022life` — Jakubowicz, Sällström, Vega, Yarahmadi, Lifetime predictions and status assessments, Energiforsk report 2022:872 (30 Jun 2022), s. 8.2: 'EN 253 (2019) states that the service life shall be 30 years at 120 °C with single peaks of 140 °C' for 300 h/yr; 50 years needs below 115 °C; degradation ×2.5 per +10 °C — https://energiforsk.se/media/31455/liftetime-predictions-and-status-assessments-energiforskrapport-2022-872.pdf
- `tbsCBA2018` — Treasury Board of Canada Secretariat, Policy on Cost-Benefit Analysis (1 Sep 2018), s. 7.1: opportunity cost of capital as the discount rate; social discount rate when impacts are long-term (50+ years) or mainly on private consumption; s. 7.4: minimum 10-year period — https://www.canada.ca/en/treasury-board-secretariat/services/federal-regulatory-management/guidelines-tools/policy-cost-benefit-analysis.html
- `scotHeat2011` — Scottish Government, The recovery of heat from power generation in Scotland (4 Nov 2011), economics: NPV at 9%, 40-year life — https://www.gov.scot/publications/study-recovery-heat-power-generation-scotland/pages/5/
- `vanNEURates2026` — City of Vancouver, 2026 False Creek Neighbourhood Energy Utility customer rates, RTS 18251 (21 Oct 2025): levelised rate approach, commercial utility model with a return on invested capital — https://council.vancouver.ca/20251112/documents/spec1d.pdf
- `nrelESIF2019` — Van Geet (NREL), Data Center Optimization Strategies: NREL ESIF Data Center Energy and Water Savings (Aug 2019): 24 °C cooling water supply, 35–40 °C return used to heat offices and lab space; ERE 0.929 (first year of TSC operation) — https://nrel.gov/docs/fy19osti/74464.pdf; NREL warm-water liquid cooling page: building needs at least 95 °F on the coldest day — https://www.nrel.gov/computational-science/warm-water-liquid-cooling
- `vanNEConnect2013` — City of Vancouver, Neighbourhood Energy Connectivity Standards bulletin (Dec 2013), s. 4.3 maximum temperatures: radiant floor 45/35 °C, panels 60/45, baseboard 70/50, fan coils 70/50, make-up air 65/45, DHW 60 °C — https://guidelines.vancouver.ca/bulletins/bulletin-sustainability-neighbourhood-energy-connectivity-standards.pdf

## Remaining gaps (after two search passes, verification, hunts 9–10)

1. ~~`free_cooling_hours` needs the CWEC hourly file~~ — resolved 10 Oct; approach temperatures remain assumptions.
2. `offerable_area` is judged (20) until the field log and permits fill it per site.
3. ~~BC long-run marginal grid factor: none published; Alberta EGDF v3.1–3.3 values to read~~ — Alberta read (v3.3 Table 1, declining 0.49 → 0.34 t/MWh 2024–2029); BC marginal factor still unpublished.
4. Operator floor price and any Canadian/US heat price paid to a data centre: not public; user session.
5. ~~Canadian summer/winter heat-demand ratio~~ — computed from CWEC (data/cwec_derived.json); building-stock weighting still unapplied.
6. Kelvion dry-cooler sound power: per-fan vs unit unresolved; Trane and Güntner values now available instead.
7. Grid headroom at the parcel: no load-capacity map in Vancouver or Edmonton; tool reports the governing document and 'stop and study' above feeder scale (sources/09).
8. Edmonton groundwater: AWWID export not yet in hand (retrieval guide F6); Vancouver GWELLS is.
9. Equinix regional cabinet utilisation: FY2024 10-K Item 7 to read manually (retrieval guide L2).
